The '''2022 United States House of Representatives elections''' were held on November 8, 2022, as part of the [[2022 United States elections]] during President [[Joe Biden]]'s term. Representatives were elected from all 435 [[U.S. congressional districts]] across each of the 50 states to serve in the [[118th United States Congress]], as well as 5 [[non-voting members of the U.S. House of Representatives]] from the [[District of Columbia]] and four of the five inhabited [[insular area]]s. Numerous other federal, state, and local elections, including the [[2022 U.S. Senate elections]] and the [[2022 U.S. gubernatorial elections]], were also held simultaneously. These were the first elections after the 2020 redistricting cycle.

The [[Republican Party (United States)|Republican Party]], led by [[Kevin McCarthy]], won control of the House, defeating [[Nancy Pelosi]] and the [[Democratic Party (United States)|Democratic Party]], which had held a majority in the House since 2019, as a result of the [[2018 United States House of Representatives elections|2018 elections]].<ref name="FiveThirtyEight 2022">{{cite web |date=November 8, 2022 |title=2022 Election: Live Analysis and Results |url=https://fivethirtyeight.com/live-blog/2022-midterm-election/ |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20221108150157/https://fivethirtyeight.com/live-blog/2022-midterm-election/ |url-status=dead |archive-date=November 8, 2022 |access-date=November 9, 2022 |website=FiveThirtyEight }}</ref><ref>{{cite web |date=November 15, 2022 |title=The Uncalled Races Of The 2022 Election: Live Updates |url=https://fivethirtyeight.com/live-blog/2022-election-house/ |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20221114181809/https://fivethirtyeight.com/live-blog/2022-election-house/ |url-status=dead |archive-date=November 14, 2022 |access-date=November 16, 2022 |website=FiveThirtyEight }}</ref><!--Already mentioned in introduction which party won the house; paragraph 3.--> Although most observers and pundits predicted large Republican gains,<ref name="Hounshell 2022">{{cite news |last=Hounshell |first=Blake |date=November 9, 2022 |title=Five Takeaways From a Red Wave That Didn't Reach the Shore |newspaper=The New York Times |url=https://www.nytimes.com/2022/11/09/us/politics/midterm-elections-takeaways.html |access-date=November 9, 2022 |issn=0362-4331}}</ref><ref name="Knowles & Scherer 2022">{{cite news |last1=Knowles |first1=Hannah |last2=Scherer |first2=Michael |date=November 9, 2022 |title=Democrats show strength, leaving fight for control of Congress unresolved |newspaper=The Washington Post |url=https://www.washingtonpost.com/elections/2022/11/08/house-senate-race/ |access-date=November 9, 2022}}</ref><ref name="McGraw 2022">{{cite web |last=McGraw |first=Meridith |date=November 9, 2022 |title=Trump's biggest midterm bets don't pay out |url=https://www.politico.com/news/2022/11/09/trump-endorsed-candidates-2022-election-results |access-date=November 9, 2022 |website=Politico}}</ref> they instead narrowly won 4 seats over the 218 seats needed for a majority,<ref>{{Cite web |title=Midterm Election Results for the House of Representatives 2022 {{!}} CNN Politics |url=https://www.cnn.com/election/2022/results/house |access-date=November 20, 2023 |website=CNN |language=en}}</ref> as Democrats won several upsets in districts considered Republican-leaning or won by [[Donald Trump]] in the [[2020 U.S. presidential election]], such as {{ushr|WA|3}}. Republicans also won some upsets in districts that Joe Biden won by double-digits, including {{ushr|NY|4}}.<ref>{{cite news|last=Yglesias|first=Matthew|date=November 9, 2022 |title=Democrats did far better than expected. How come?|url= https://www.theguardian.com/commentisfree/2022/nov/09/democrats-did-far-better-than-expected-how-come |access-date=November 9, 2022 |work=The Guardian }}</ref><ref>{{cite web |last=Rakich |first=Nathaniel |date=November 16, 2022 |title=Republicans Won The House — Barely |url=https://fivethirtyeight.com/features/house-control-republicans/ |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20221117001841/https://fivethirtyeight.com/features/house-control-republicans/ |url-status=dead |archive-date=November 17, 2022 |access-date=November 16, 2022 |website=FiveThirtyEight}}</ref> Observers attributed Democrats' surprise over-performance to, among other factors,<ref name="Koerth 2022">{{cite web |last=Koerth |first=Maggie |date=November 18, 2022 |title=So You Think You Can Explain The Election |url=https://fivethirtyeight.com/features/election-explanations-are-hard/ |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20221118152513/https://fivethirtyeight.com/features/election-explanations-are-hard/ |url-status=dead |archive-date=November 18, 2022 |access-date=November 20, 2022 |website=FiveThirtyEight}}</ref> the issue of [[abortion in the United States]] after ''[[Dobbs v. Jackson Women's Health Organization]]'',<ref name="Radcliffe 2022">{{cite web |last=Radcliffe |first=Mary |date=November 17, 2022 |title=Abortion Was Always Going To Impact The Midterms |url=https://fivethirtyeight.com/features/abortion-was-always-going-to-impact-the-midterms/ |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20221117110110/https://fivethirtyeight.com/features/abortion-was-always-going-to-impact-the-midterms/ |url-status=dead |archive-date=November 17, 2022 |access-date=November 20, 2022 |website=FiveThirtyEight}}</ref> and the underperformance of multiple statewide and congressional Republican candidates who held extreme views,<ref name="Bender & Haberman 2022">{{cite news |last1=Bender |first1=Michael C. |last2=Haberman |first2=Maggie |date=November 10, 2022 |title=Trump Under Fire From Within G.O.P. After Midterms |newspaper=The New York Times |url=https://www.nytimes.com/2022/11/09/us/politics/trump-republicans-midterms.html |access-date=November 30, 2022 |issn=0362-4331}}</ref><ref name="Enten 2022">{{cite news |last=Enten |first=Harry |date=November 13, 2022 |title=How Joe Biden and the Democratic Party defied midterm history |url=https://www.cnn.com/2022/11/13/politics/democrats-biden-midterm-elections-senate-house/index.html |access-date=November 28, 2022 |publisher=CNN}}</ref><ref name="Wolf 2022">{{cite news |last=Wolf |first=Zachary B. |date=November 14, 2022 |title=These Republicans are admitting the party has an extremism problem |publisher=CNN |url=https://edition.cnn.com/2022/11/14/politics/republican-party-extremism-problem-what-matters/index.html |access-date=November 30, 2022}}</ref> including [[Big lie#Donald Trump's false claims of a stolen election|refusal to accept the party's 2020 electoral loss]].<ref name="Dougall 2022">{{cite web |last=Dougall |first=David Mac |date=November 9, 2022 |title=US midterm elections: What have we learned so far? |url=https://www.euronews.com/2022/11/09/us-midterm-elections-what-did-we-learn-so-far |access-date=November 11, 2022 |website=Euronews }}</ref><ref>{{cite web |last=Rogers |first=Kaleigh |date=November 10, 2022 |title=Denying The 2020 Election Wasn't A Winning Strategy For Political Newcomers |url=https://fivethirtyeight.com/features/denying-the-2020-election-wasnt-a-winning-strategy-for-political-newcomers/ |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20221110215413/https://fivethirtyeight.com/features/denying-the-2020-election-wasnt-a-winning-strategy-for-political-newcomers/ |url-status=dead |archive-date=November 10, 2022 |access-date=November 11, 2022 |website=FiveThirtyEight}}</ref> On the other hand, Democrats' political prospects were weighed down by the [[2021–2022 inflation spike]], which Republicans blamed on President Biden and the Democratic-controlled Congress.<ref>{{Cite web |last=Luhby |first=Ariel Edwards-Levy, Tami |date=November 8, 2022 |title=Exit polls: High inflation dominates voters' views in the midterm elections {{!}} CNN Politics |url=https://www.cnn.com/2022/11/08/politics/exit-polls-midterm-election-analysis/index.html |access-date=January 11, 2023 |website=CNN |language=en}}</ref> The elections marked the first time since [[1874–75 United States House of Representatives elections|1875]] that Democrats won all districts along the Pacific Ocean.<ref>{{cite news |last=Mishanec |first=Nora |date=November 22, 2022 |title=Democrats now control all House seats along the Pacific Ocean for the first time in memory |url=https://www.sfchronicle.com/bayarea/article/After-key-California-victories-Democrats-control-17604460.php |work=San Francisco Chronicle|access-date=December 2, 2022}}</ref> This was the first time since [[2004 United States House of Representatives elections|2004]] that Republicans gained House seats in consecutive elections.

[[Gerrymandering]] during the [[2020 U.S. redistricting cycle]] had a significant impact on the 2022 election results. Republicans made gains as a result of gerrymandering in Florida, Georgia, Tennessee, and Texas, while Democrats made gains as a result of gerrymandering in Illinois and New Mexico. Defensive gerrymanders helped both parties hold competitive seats in various states,<ref>{{cite web |last=Donnini |first=Zachary |date=November 29, 2022 |title=Republicans Are Developing a Geography Problem in the US House |url=https://decisiondeskhq.com/the-us-house-was-biased-toward-democrats-in-2022-why-republicans-are-developing-a-geography-problem/ |access-date=December 11, 2022 |website=Decision Desk HQ |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20240324061805/https://decisiondeskhq.com/the-us-house-was-biased-toward-democrats-in-2022-why-republicans-are-developing-a-geography-problem/ |archive-date=March 24, 2024 |url-status=dead}}</ref> while Republican gains in New York and Democratic gains in North Carolina and Ohio were made possible because their [[state supreme court]]s overturned gerrymanders passed by their [[State legislature (United States)|state legislatures]].<ref name="FiveThirtyEight 2022"/><ref name="Meja & Rakich 2022">{{cite web |last1=Mejia |first1=Elena |last2=Rakich |first2=Nathaniel |date=December 1, 2022 |title=Did Redistricting Cost Democrats The House? |url=https://fivethirtyeight.com/features/redistricting-house-2022/ |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20221201112945/https://fivethirtyeight.com/features/redistricting-house-2022/ |url-status=dead |archive-date=December 1, 2022 |access-date=December 1, 2022 |website=FiveThirtyEight}}</ref><ref>{{cite news |url=https://www.cnbc.com/2022/11/16/midterm-house-elections-2022-republicans-take-control-of-the-house.html |date=November 16, 2022 |first=Kevin |last=Breuninger |title=Republicans take control of the House, NBC News projects |website=CNBC |access-date=November 16, 2022 }}</ref><ref>{{cite web |last1=Burnett|first1=Sara|last2=Colvin|first2=Jill|last3=Weissert|first3=Will|date=November 16, 2022 |title=Republicans win back control of House with narrow majority |url=https://apnews.com/article/2022-midterm-elections-house-control-79475a4fc11e4375cd0dded651b9eede |access-date=November 30, 2022 |website=AP News |publisher=Associated Press}}</ref>

The narrow margin by which Republicans won their House majority resulted in historic legislative difficulties in the [[118th United States Congress|118th Congress]]. Due to a number of Republican holdouts affiliated with the right-wing [[Freedom Caucus|House Freedom Caucus]], McCarthy was not [[January 2023 Speaker of the United States House of Representatives election|elected]] Speaker of the House until the 15th round of voting, thus marking the first time since [[1923 Speaker of the United States House of Representatives election|1923]] that a speaker was not elected in the first round.<ref>{{Cite news |last=Karni |first=Annie |date=January 6, 2023 |title=McCarthy Wins Speakership on 15th Vote After Concessions to Hard Right |language=en-US |work=The New York Times |url=https://www.nytimes.com/2023/01/06/us/politics/house-speaker-vote-mccarthy.html |access-date=October 4, 2023 |issn=0362-4331}}</ref> This was the smallest Republican majority since [[2000 United States House of Representatives elections|2000]], before Republicans were re-elected with a smaller majority in [[2024 United States House of Representatives elections|2024]].

== Electoral system ==
Forty-six states used the [[first-past-the-post voting]] plurality system to elect their representatives. [[Instant-runoff voting]] was used in two states (Alaska and Maine) and [[Two-round system|runoff system]] was used in two states (Georgia and Louisiana).

== Results ==
As [[mail-in voting]] has increased in U.S. elections, particularly since the [[COVID-19 pandemic]], the results in some congressional races were not known immediately following the election, which was more competitive and closer than expected,<ref>{{cite news |last1=Ax |first1=Joseph|last2=Reid|first2=Tim |date=November 10, 2022 |title=U.S. Senate up for grabs as Republicans move toward House majority |work=Reuters |url=https://www.reuters.com/world/us/republicans-close-us-house-majority-senate-still-up-grabs-2022-11-10/ |access-date=November 10, 2022}}</ref> as a widely predicted [[red wave election]] did not materialize.<ref>{{cite web |last=Siders |first=David |date=November 9, 2022|title=The red wave that wasn't: 5 takeaways from a disappointing night for the GOP |url=https://www.politico.com/news/2022/11/09/2022-election-results-analysis-and-takeaways-00065878 |access-date=December 1, 2022 |website=Politico}}</ref> Instead, Democrats lost fewer seats than expected at less than 10 and fewer than the average (25) for the president's party since the end of World War II.<ref>{{cite web |last=Rakich |first=Nathaniel |date=November 17, 2022 2022 |access-date=November 30, 2022 |website=FiveThirtyEight}}</ref> and [[red states]] became redder.<ref>{{cite news|last=Brownstein |first=Ronald |date=November 14, 2022 |title=While Democrats may during the [[2020 U.S. redistricting cycle]] gave each party advantages in various states; due to advantageous maps, Republicans performed well or made gains in Florida, Georgia, Texas, and Tennessee, and Democrats made gains in Illinois and New Mexico.<ref name="FiveThirtyEight 2022"/><ref name="Meja & Rakich 2022"/> As of November 10, 14 seats were flipped, with Republicans gaining 11 of them for a net gain of 8 seats; Republicans needed to maintain a net gain of at least 5 seats to regain the House.<ref name="FiveThirtyEight 2022"/> Republicans won the popular vote by a 3 percent margin and would have won it even if Democrats had contested more seats than they did, which may have cost them about 1–2 percent in the final popular vote margin.<ref name="Meja & Rakich 2022"/> According to [[Harry Enten]] of [[CNN]], the final popular vote margin was the second-closest midterm margin for a U.S. House election in the last 70 years.<ref>{{cite news|url=https://www.cnn.com/2022/12/26/politics/midterm-election-2022-historically-close/index.html|title=The most underdiscussed fact of the 2022 election: how historically close it was|publisher=CNN|last=Enten|first=Harry|date=December 26, 2022|access-date=December 26, 2022}}</ref>

The unprecedented degree of Republican underperformance during the election defied election analysts' predictions of heavy gains, given that while a majority of voters trusted Democrats on abortion, they were disappointed with the performance of [[Joe Biden]] and Democrats on issues facing the country, such as the economy and inflation, crime, and immigration.<ref name="Hounshell 2022"/><ref name="Knowles & Scherer 2022"/><ref name="McGraw 2022"/> This has been variously attributed by political commentators to the issue of abortion after ''[[Dobbs v. Jackson Women's Health Organization]]'' overturned ''[[Roe v. Wade]]'' in June 2022;<ref name="Radcliffe 2022"/> candidate quality among Republicans who held [[extremist]] or unpopular views,<ref name="Bender & Haberman 2022"/><ref name="Enten 2022"/><ref name="Wolf 2022"/> such as [[denial of the 2020 U.S. presidential election]] results;<ref name="Dougall 2022"/> and youth turnout, among others.<ref name="Koerth 2022"/> According to [[Ron Brownstein]] of CNN in 2023, exit polls showed that House Democrats won independent voters by 2 percentage points, making it the first time the party holding the White House did so in a midterm election since at least [[1982 United States House of Representatives elections|1982]].<ref>{{cite news|url=https://www.cnn.com/2023/10/10/politics/fault-lines-mccarthy-trump-speakership-2024/index.html|title=McCarthy's fall and Trump's rise reflect the same bet among Republicans|publisher=CNN|last1=Brownstein|first1=Ronald|date=October 10, 2023|access-date=October 10, 2023}}</ref>

Biden described the results as a "strong night" for Democrats,<ref>{{cite web |last=Kinery |first=Emma |date=November 9, 2022 |title=Midterm results are looking increasingly sunny for Biden as he touts 'strong night' for Democrats |url=https://www.cnbc.com/2022/11/09/midterm-election-results-look-better-for-biden-as-democrats-avoid-red-wave.html |access-date=November 10, 2022 |website=CNBC}}</ref> and he urged for cooperation in Congress.<ref name="Kestler-D'Amours & Najjar 2022">{{cite news |last1=Kestler-D'Amours|first1=Jillian|last2=Najjar|first2=Farah |date=November 9, 2022|title=Biden urges cooperation in next Congress after divisive midterms |url=https://www.aljazeera.com/news/liveblog/2022/11/9/us-midterm-elections-live-news-eyes-on-key-races-for-senate-house |access-date=November 22, 2022 |publisher=Al Jazeera}}</ref> Senator [[Lindsey Graham]] commented: "It's certainly not a red wave, that's for darn sure. But it is clear that we will take back the House."<ref>{{cite web |date=November 9, 2022 |title=US elections: the Republicans do not break through, the Senate in the balance. Biden: 'Back in the running in 2024' |url=https://news.italy24.press/world/184372.html |access-date=November 10, 2022 |website=Italy 24 Press News }}</ref> On November 9, when the results for the House were still uncertain, the Republican House leader [[Kevin McCarthy]] launched his bid to succeed long-time House Democratic leader [[Nancy Pelosi]] as [[Speaker of the U.S. House of Representatives]].<ref name="Kestler-D'Amours & Najjar 2022"/> In a letter asking for support among Republicans, he wrote: "I trust you know that earning the majority is only the beginning. Now, we will be measured by what we do with our majority. Now the real work begins."<ref name="Kestler-D'Amours & Najjar 2022"/>

Control of the House would not be known until November 16, after [[Mike Garcia (politician)|Mike Garcia]] was projected to win reelection in {{ushr|CA|27}}, giving Republicans a total of at least 218 seats;<ref name="Kinnard 2022">{{cite web | last=Kinnard|first=Meg|title=Why AP has called control of the US House for Republicans | website=AP News | publisher =Associated Press| date=November 17, 2022 | url=https://apnews.com/article/house-control-republicans-2022-election-explained-947add904c9372b99869773332898076 | access-date=November 19, 2022}}</ref> and giving Republicans a narrow majority.<ref>{{cite web |last=Kapur|first=Sahil|date=November 17, 2022|title=Republican infighting escalates over poor 2022 election results as Trump re-emerges |url=https://www.nbcnews.com/politics/2022-election/republican-infighting-escalates-poor-2022-election-results-trump-re-em-rcna57540 |access-date=November 30, 2022 |website=NBC News}}</ref> The size of the majority remained in doubt with several races still to be called more than one week after Election Day.<ref>{{cite news | title=Tracking the Remaining House Election Results and Race Calls | work=The New York Times | date=November 10, 2022 | url=https://www.nytimes.com/interactive/2022/11/10/us/elections/results-house-seats-elections-congress.html | access-date=November 19, 2022}}</ref> On November 17, after Republicans were projected to win back the House, Pelosi announced that she would not seek reelection as Speaker of the House,<ref>{{cite news |last=Smith|first=David|date=November 17, 2022 |title=Pelosi to depart as top House Democrat to make way for 'new generation'|url=https://www.theguardian.com/us-news/2022/nov/17/nancy-pelosi-house-speaker-speech-steps-down |access-date=November 23, 2022 |work=The Guardian}}</ref> and [[Hakeem Jeffries]] was later selected as the Democratic nominee by acclamation.<ref>{{cite web |last=Mascaro|first=Lisa|date=November 30, 2022 |title=Jeffries wins historic bid to lead House Dems after Pelosi |url=https://apnews.com/article/nancy-pelosi-hakeem-jeffries-congress-government-and-politics-e6dee438b2cfd776b70ff29495072ab5 |website=AP News|access-date=December 1, 2022}}</ref> On November 15, McCarthy won an internal Republican caucus poll as the party's nominee for Speaker of the House;<ref name="Kinnard 2022"/> as several members of the Republican caucus did not vote for him and had expressed opposition to his speakership, it cast doubt on how the [[January 2023 Speaker of the United States House of Representatives election|2023 U.S. speaker election]], which began on January 3, would unfold.<ref>{{cite web|last=Brooks|first=Emily|work=The Hill|title=Whip List: McCarthy searches for 218 GOP Speakership votes|date=November 25, 2022|url=https://thehill.com/homenews/house/3748648-whip-list-mccarthy-searches-for-218-gop-speakership-votes/|access-date=November 30, 2022}}</ref><ref>{{cite web |last=Rakich |first=Nathaniel |date=December 9, 2022 |title=How Kevin McCarthy Could Lose The Election For Speaker Of The House |url=https://fivethirtyeight.com/features/house-speaker-kevin-mccarthy-votes/ |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20221209110149/https://fivethirtyeight.com/features/house-speaker-kevin-mccarthy-votes/ |url-status=dead |archive-date=December 9, 2022 |access-date=December 9, 2022 |website=FiveThirtyEight}}</ref> McCarthy's speaker bid was the first of a party leader [[List of Speaker of the United States House of Representatives elections#December 1923|since 1923]] that did not succeed on the first ballot.

== Retirements ==
[[File:2022 United States House of Representatives elections retirements or losses of renomination map.svg|thumb|350px|Retiring incumbents by district<br />{{legend0|#92C5DE|Democratic incumbent ran}}<br />{{legend0|#0671B0|Democratic incumbent retired or lost renomination}}<br />{{legend0|#F48882|Republican incumbent ran}}<br />{{legend0|#CA0120|Republican incumbent retired or lost renomination}}<br />{{legend0|#BB91B1|Democratic and Republican incumbent ran}}<br />{{legend0|#6D6D6D|Vacant or new district}}]]
In total, 49 representatives and one non-voting delegate (30 Democrats and 20 Republicans) retired, 17 of whom (nine Democrats and eight Republicans) sought other offices.<ref name=":1a">{{cite web |title=Casualty List |url=https://pressgallery.house.gov/member-data/casualty-list |website=U.S. House of Representatives Press Gallery |access-date=December 30, 2021}}</ref>

==Resignations and death==
Three seats were left vacant on the day of the general election due to resignations or death in 2022, two of which were not filled until the next Congress.

===Democrats===
Two Democrats resigned before the end of their terms.
# {{ushr|FL|13|x}}: [[Charlie Crist]] resigned August 31 to [[2022 Florida gubernatorial election|run for governor of Florida]].<ref name="Crist">{{cite web |last=Dixon |first=Matt |date=August 31, 2022 |title=Charlie Crist resigns from Congress as race for Florida governor ramps up |url=https://www.politico.com/news/2022/08/31/charlie-crist-resigns-from-congress-as-general-election-begins-00054328 |access-date=August 31, 2022 |website=Politico |language=en-US}}</ref>
# {{ushr|FL|22|x}}: [[Ted Deutch]] resigned September 30 to become CEO of the [[American Jewish Committee]].<ref name=Deutch>{{cite web|last=Man|first=Anthony|title=Exiting Congress early, Ted Deutch assesses wins, losses — and increasingly toxic politics|url=https://www.sun-sentinel.com/news/politics/fl-ne-ted-deutch-exits-congress-american-jewish-committee-20220930-x3nfgiv5brexnbgvvhneohh27m-story.html|publisher=Orlando Sun-Sentinel|date=September 30, 2022|access-date=September 30, 2022}}</ref>

===Republicans===
One Republican died in office.
# {{ushr|IN|2|x}}: [[Jackie Walorski]] died August 3. A [[2022 Indiana's 2nd congressional district special election|special election]] to fill the remainder of her term was held concurrently with the general election for the next full term.<ref name=WalorskiDeath />

== Incumbents defeated ==
Fourteen incumbents lost renomination in the primary elections and nine incumbents lost reelection in the general elections.

=== In primary elections ===
==== Democrats ====
Six Democrats, half of whom were freshmen, lost renomination.

# {{ushr|GA|7|X}}: [[Carolyn Bourdeaux]] (first elected in 2020) lost a redistricting race to fellow incumbent [[Lucy McBath]], who won the general election.<ref>{{Cite news|last=Mitchell|first=Tia|title=Lucy McBath defeats Carolyn Bourdeaux in Georgia's 7th District primary|url=https://www.ajc.com/politics/lucy-mcbath-defeats-carolyn-bourdeaux-in-georgias-7th-district-primary/MBNWFGF2X5HA7MHXLKUZGTFLHI/|date=May 24, 2022|website=[[The Atlanta Journal-Constitution]]|access-date=May 26, 2022}}</ref>
# {{ushr|IL|6|X}}: [[Marie Newman]] (first elected in 2020) lost a redistricting race to fellow incumbent [[Sean Casten]], who won the general election.<ref>{{Cite web |last=Greenwood |first=Max |date=June 29, 2022 |title=Rep. Casten defeats fellow Democratic Rep. Newman in Illinois primary |url=https://thehill.com/homenews/campaign/3540130-rep-casten-defeats-fellow-democratic-rep-newman-in-illinois-primary/ |access-date=June 29, 2022 |website=The Hill |language=en-US}}</ref>
# {{ushr|MI|11|X}}: [[Andy Levin]] (first elected in 2018) lost a redistricting race to fellow incumbent [[Haley Stevens]], who won the general election.<ref>{{cite web|last=Allen|first=Jonathan|title=Rep. Haley Stevens ends Levin political dynasty in brutal Democratic primary|url=https://www.nbcnews.com/news/rcna40662|website=NBC News|date=August 2, 2022|access-date=August 2, 2022}}</ref>

==Reapportionment==
{{see also|2020 United States redistricting cycle|United States congressional apportionment|Redistricting in the United States}}
The [[2020 United States census]] determined how many of the 435 congressional districts each state receives for the 2020 redistricting cycle. Due to population shifts, California, Illinois, Michigan, New York, Ohio, Pennsylvania, and West Virginia each lost one seat. Conversely, Colorado, Florida, Montana, North Carolina, and Oregon each gained one seat; and Texas gained two seats.<ref>{{cite news|url=https://www.cnn.com/2021/04/26/politics/us-census-2020-results/index.html|title=Census Bureau announces 331 million people in US, Texas will add two congressional seats|work=CNN|last1=Merica|first1=Dan|last2=Stark|first2=Liz|date=April 26, 2021|access-date=April 26, 2021}}</ref>

== Vulnerable seats ==
This is a list of House seats where the winner of the [[2020 United States presidential election|2020 presidential election]] and the incumbent in the district were from different parties. The results for the 2020 elections accounted for redistricting and was representative of the new district boundaries.<ref>{{Cite web |title=Daily Kos Elections' 2020 presidential results by congressional district, for new and old districts |url=https://www.dailykos.com/story/2021/9/29/2055001/-Daily-Kos-Elections-2020-presidential-results-by-congressional-district-for-new-and-old-districts |access-date=November 13, 2022 |website=Daily Kos}}</ref>

# [[Pennsylvania's 8th congressional district|Pennsylvania 8]] (Trump +2.9, [[Matt Cartwright]] (D) won re-election)

== Election ratings ==
{{Main|2022 United States House of Representatives election ratings}}

In February 2022, ''[[The Guardian]]'' reported that "America is poised to have a staggeringly low number of competitive seats in the US House, an alarming trend that makes it harder to govern and exacerbates political polarization." The 2020 redistricting cycle resulted in 94% of the U.S. House running in relatively safe seats, often due to [[gerrymandering]].<ref>{{cite news |last=Levine |first=Sam |title=America faces greater division as parties draw safe seats for congressional districts |url=https://www.theguardian.com/us-news/ng-interactive/2022/feb/12/us-redistricting-house-seats-safe-competitive-districts |access-date=February 17, 2022 |work=The Guardian |date=February 12, 2022 }}</ref><ref>{{Cite web |last3=Rakich |first1=Ryan |last1=Best |first2=Aaron |last2=Bycoffe |first3=Nathaniel |date=August 9, 2021 |title=What Redistricting Looks Like In Every State |url=https://projects.fivethirtyeight.com/redistricting-2022-maps/ |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20210809100425/https://projects.fivethirtyeight.com/redistricting-2022-maps/ |url-status=dead |archive-date=August 9, 2021 |access-date=June 4, 2022 |website=FiveThirtyEight}}</ref>
